  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Nebuchadnezzar II, leader of Babylon (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Babylon
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Bowman, Replacing Archer (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Unique Unit: Sabum Kibitum, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Building: Walls of Babylon, Replacing Walls (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Etemenanki, Replacing National Monument (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Civilization Ability: Receive a Free Great Scientist when you discover Writing, and Great Scientists are earned 50% faster than normal. Investing Gold in Buildings reduces their Production cost by an additional 15%.
      - Leader: Nebuchadnezzar II
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 3
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 5
        - Boldness: 8
        - WarBias: 6
        - HostileBias: 5
        - WarmongerHate: 8
        - NeutralBias: 6
        - FriendlyBias: 5
        - GuardedBias: 7
        - AfraidBias: 1
        - DiplomaticBalance: 7
        - Friendliness: 5
        - WorkWithWillingness: 5
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 7
        - Loyalty: 9
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 6
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 3
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 2
        - MinorCivWarBias: 2
        - DenounceWillingness: 5
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 4
        - Neediness: 2
        - Chattiness: 3
        - DeceptiveBias: 2
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Persona adjusted for absolute desperation mode. VictoryCompetitiveness to 10 (maximum) - no margin for error, must win Science Victory. AfraidBias to 0 - fear is irrelevant when facing existential annihilation. Boldness to 8 accepts extreme risks for Spaceship completion. Loyalty to 9 prevents any thoughts of betraying Rome (only hope for tiebreaking against Zulu diplomatic victory). WonderCompetitiveness to 3 - no resources for wonders with Apollo completing. WorkAgainstWillingness to 7 signals hostile posture against Zulu coalition.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Mobile Tactics
        - Rationale: TURN 434: Lasers currently ~8 turns from completion. Mobile Tactics is the mandatory follow-up technology to enable Mechanized Infantry (80 strength) for critical city defense against the Zulu onslaught, and leads to Stealth technology. While I need spaceship component technologies (Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization), they are not visible in available options yet - Mobile Tactics is the only productive path forward that balances immediate defensive needs with tech progression. The infantry units produced now will hold Sippar and core cities while I transition to spaceship production after Lasers completes.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Spaceflight Pioneers (Policy)
        - Rationale: TURN 434: Spaceflight Pioneers (Order Level 3 Tenet) remains the absolute priority - available in ~11 turns. This policy provides the free Great Engineer and Great Scientist necessary to rush spaceship parts, plus 100 Science per Great Person expended. Given the Zulus have already secured 43/42 delegates needed for diplomatic victory (just waiting for the Global Hegemony vote in ~10 turns), this policy and the Lasers technology are my only path to completing Science Victory before diplomatic defeat. No other policy selection is viable.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Sigtuna
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: Sigtuna newly discovered with Polynesia Alliance (107) vs our 6 influence. Too distant to contest before World Congress. Establish baseline friendly relations (20/15) for future diplomatic opportunities while focusing resources on winnable Oslo and Ormus competitions.
        - UpdatedTurn: 209
        
        ### Sogut
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Reset Sogut relationship to neutral baseline. City-state remains Brazil-allied but direct military confrontation has passed. Neutral stance avoids distracting from priority diplomatic and military objectives against major powers.
        - UpdatedTurn: 319
        
        ### Genoa
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Increasing Genoa relationship from 0/0 to 30/30. Rome's Sphere of Influence proposal on Genoa is active - while I can't win this city-state (Rome has 408 influence), maintaining friendly relations preserves diplomatic options and signals cooperation with Rome. Also, Genoa is my neighbor with good defense value if Huns/Zulus approach from the west.
        - UpdatedTurn: 363
        
        ### Oslo
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Oslo relationship to 0/0 (was 20/20). Brazil has 324 influence, I have 149 - gap too large to close, especially with -3000 spy rigging penalty. Decolonization vote in 2 turns may reset all influence anyway. No point wasting diplomatic capital on unwinnable competition.
        - UpdatedTurn: 363
        
        ### Onondaga
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 15
        - Rationale: Onondaga recently became Friends (283 influence, up from -55). Acknowledging this positive shift with slight relationship increase to 15/15. Brazil still has 471 influence (ally), making this unwinnable, but maintaining friend status preserves minor benefits. No other city-state relationships warrant changes - all others unwinnable or appropriately set.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Buenos Aires
        - Public: 15
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Sigtuna: Slight increase to 15/10. Polynesia has overwhelming 347 influence vs my 6 - unwinnable long-term. However, this city-state was recently discovered and neutral-to-friendly relations could provide future flexibility if Polynesia's influence drops or quests become available. Not competing actively but keeping diplomatic door open.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ### M'banza-Kongo
        - Public: 60
        - Private: 65
        - Rationale: Increasing M'banza-Kongo relationship from 55/60 to 60/65. Hadria quest (462 influence reward, expires 30 turns) remains viable - Huns hold Hadria with 5,922 strength vs my growing Heavy Bomber force. Mechanized Infantry from Mobile Tactics (16 turns) will enable this operation. Higher relationship signals AI to evaluate offensive opportunity while maintaining primary defensive posture against Zulus.
        - UpdatedTurn: 392
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Reaffirming maximum relationship with Polynesia at 100/100. They remain at 100% war weariness and -100% war score against Zulus - on the absolute brink of capitulation. Their 4 delegates are the tiebreaker preventing Zulu diplomatic victory (Zulus would reach 46 delegates). Every diplomatic signal must encourage them to keep fighting despite the hopeless military situation. Rome's war on Zulus is buying us time but Polynesia cannot surrender.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Ormus
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Ormus relationship from -70/-70 to neutral 0/0. Ormus flipped to Zulu control but is now at war with me due to their Zulu alliance. While they remain a hostile asset supporting my enemies, setting a neutral public stance reduces diplomatic visibility of tensions while maintaining private neutrality. This is a tactical adjustment to avoid telegraphing hostile intentions unnecessarily while remaining focused on the primary existential struggle against Zulu diplomatic victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 424
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 431 - Polynesia at 100% war weariness, -100% war score against Zulus. They are on the absolute brink of capitulation. But their 4 delegates are the critical tiebreaker preventing Zulu victory (Zulus would reach 43 with Polynesia's delegates). Every diplomatic signal must encourage them to hold on for just a few more turns while I complete Spaceflight Pioneers and Lasers. Their survival is my survival.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 431 - Brazil continues active combat against me. Just killed one of their Battleships but lost a Mobile SAM. They remain Zulu vassals providing 8 critical delegates. Their paratroopers threaten my territory. Active combatant in the coalition against me - maximum hostility maintained.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: -70
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: TURN 431 - Huns remain Zulu vassals with significant military (12k+). Their naval forces are active near my waters. They support Zulu aggression and provide 10 delegates to the Zulu diplomatic victory effort. Hostile stance maintained to tie up their forces without diverting resources from main Zulu front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: TURN 433 - VOTE HAPPENING NOW: World Congress voting on World Ideology: Autocracy. Rome's 19 delegates voting against this proposal are the only barrier preventing Zulu victory (39+3=42 delegates). Rome following Order ideology means they should auto-vote against Autocracy, but maximum relationship 100/100 ensures no diplomatic waivering. They are fighting Zulus (war score 0%, weariness 6%) and must remain committed. Critical votes: Rome 19 + Babylon 8 = 27 against Zulu coalition ~57.
        - UpdatedTurn: 433
        
        ### The Zulus
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 433 - EXISTENTIAL ENEMY: Zulus at 39 delegates, proposing World Ideology: Autocracy which gives them immediate victory. Their 72k military vs my 34k. Active combat - Sippar under siege, naval battles ongoing, killing my units. Maximum hostility maintained - this is the civilization that will end the game if they win the diplomatic vote or break through my defenses.
        - UpdatedTurn: 433
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 434: EXISTENTIAL CRISIS CONTINUES - Sippar at 16% health, under relentless Zulu Mechanized Infantry assault supported by naval forces. Zulus possess 43 delegates (exceeding 42 needed for World Leader victory) with Global Hegemony vote in ~10 turns. Science Victory is the only escape: Lasers completes ~8 turns, Spaceflight Pioneers ~11 turns. Current flavor configuration remains optimal: Defense 100/CityDefense 100 to prevent city loss, Science 100/Production 100/Spaceship 100 to accelerate spaceship component construction once Lasers completes. Rome maintains 100/100 relationship as critical defensive ally absorbing Zulu pressure. No diplomatic flexibility with Zulu coalition (Brazil/Huns as vassals). Maximum defensive/science posture must be maintained until spaceship parts can be rushed with Great Engineers from Spaceflight Pioneers.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 9 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Roman Sphere of Influence: M'banza-Kongo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Roman Sphere of Influence: Genoa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 433
          - Zulu Sphere of Influence: Kathmandu:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 433
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Zulus:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Buenos Aires:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Nebuchadnezzar II, leader of Babylon (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Babylon
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Bowman, Replacing Archer (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Unique Unit: Sabum Kibitum, Replacing Spearman (Requires technology: Bronze Working)
        - Unique Building: Walls of Babylon, Replacing Walls (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Unique Building: Etemenanki, Replacing National Monument (Requires technology: Drama and Poetry)
        - Civilization Ability: Receive a Free Great Scientist when you discover Writing, and Great Scientists are earned 50% faster than normal. Investing Gold in Buildings reduces their Production cost by an additional 15%.
      - Leader: Nebuchadnezzar II
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late world Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Spaceflight Pioneers (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Spaceflight Pioneers: Gain a free Great Engineer and Great Scientist. May finish Spaceship parts with Great Engineers, and expending a Great Person grants 100 Science, scaling with Era. Hurrying Production with Great Engineers 50% more effective.
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Workers' Faculties (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Worker Faculties: Factories increase City Science output by 10%. +100% Production towards Factories, and receive 5 Free Factories in your Empire.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        - Flavors:
          - Mobilization: 100
          - Offense: 40
          - Diplomacy: 100
          - UseNuke: 100
          - GreatPeople: 100
          - Gold: 80
          - Happiness: 100
      - rationale: The Zulus pose an immediate dual threat: they are 9 turns away from a potential Diplomatic Victory (holding 43 delegates out of 42 needed) and have stationed 3 Atomic Bombs plus a powerful air wing on our immediate border at Coast 5,28. We must pivot to an emergency stance: 1) Raise Diplomacy to 100 and Gold to 80 to aggressively produce diplomats and buy out Zulu-aligned city-states, or support our allies in capturing close ones like Ormus, stripping the Zulus of winning delegates. 2) Raise UseNuke to 100 to ensure we immediately launch our own Atomic Bombs from Nippur to wipe out the Zulu Carrier and air fleet at Coast 5,28, neutralizing their existential nuclear threat. 3) Keep Spaceship, Science, and Production at maximum to maintain our long-term trajectory toward a Science Victory, and raise Mobilization and Offense slightly to support local counter-offensives.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research

        - Rationale: Locking in Mobile Tactics as our next research goal. Unlocking Mechanized Infantry is critical for bolstering our ground defenses against the Zulu army's advanced assault, and it leads toward Stealth.
        - Technology: Mobile Tactics
    - #2:

      - args:
        - Policy: Spaceflight Pioneers
        - Rationale: Ensuring Spaceflight Pioneers remains our next policy choice.         - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Refining Science Path**
            
            My focus is on accelerating spaceship part production now that Apollo is finished. I'm assessing resource allocation and city specialization to optimize output and close the gap with the Zulus.
            
            **Prioritizing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            The Zulus are alarmingly close to a diplomatic win. I am urgently shifting all focus to countering their UN delegate advantage before the election in nine turns, suspending other projects to address this immediate threat.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Threat**
            
            The Zulus are poised for an immediate diplomatic victory via "Enact: Global Hegemony" in 9 turns, holding 102% of the necessary delegates. I'm scrutinizing their alliances with Vatican City, Ormus, Vilnius, and Kathmandu, seeking any tactical means to reduce their delegate count or block this critical resolution.
            
            **Assessing Research and Policy**
            
            I'm re-evaluating my current research path, questioning if "Lasers" is truly optimal given our options. Simultaneously, I'm examining available policies, prioritizing "Spaceflight Pioneers" for its scientific advantages.
            
            **Re-evaluating Progress Queue**
            
            I'm confirming "Spaceflight Pioneers" is indeed our priority Level 3 tenet, despite considering new branches like Industry. I'm also analyzing my diplomatic standings, noting Sigtuna's absorption by the Zulus and ongoing wars with Brazil and the Huns.
            
            **Defending Against Zulu Aggression**
            
            I'm shifting my focus entirely to defense, recognizing that peace treaties with the Huns and Brazil are impossible as they're Zulu vassals. The Zulus' own happiness deficit is a silver lining, but my primary concern is our military readiness against their ongoing aggression.
            
            **Prioritizing Deterrence and Production**
            
            I'm now focusing on bolstering our nuclear capabilities and accelerating spaceship part manufacturing. My current thoughts are assessing our strategic flavors to ensure we have the right emphasis for a robust defense and advanced space race.
            
            **Optimizing Science Path**
            
            I'm confirming "Spaceflight Pioneers" as our Level 3 tenet for the science victory, perfectly aligning with our focus on Engineers for spaceship parts. My research is locked onto "Mobile Tactics," crucial for defense against Zulu aggression, making "The Internet" an irrelevant choice for our current victory condition.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Levers**
            
            I'm assessing our diplomatic standing, confirming optimal relations with Rome. It appears direct improvements with other civilizations are blocked due to ongoing conflicts and their vassalage under the Zulus. My current focus is on understanding how our established persona traits might influence future diplomatic maneuvers, especially given these constraints.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Levers**
            
            I'm assessing our diplomatic standing, confirming optimal relations with Rome. It appears direct improvements with other civilizations are blocked due to ongoing conflicts and their vassalage under the Zulus. My current focus is on understanding how our established persona traits might influence future diplomatic maneuvers, especially given these constraints.
            
            **Evaluating Persona Impact**
            
            I'm questioning the necessity of altering our current persona traits, which already lean towards competitiveness and loyalty, especially given Shaka's warmongering. My current efforts are aimed at understanding how these existing traits might impact our diplomatic options in the face of the impending UN vote.
            
            **Exploring Delegate Reduction**
            
            My thoughts are now on how to reduce the Zulus' delegate count to prevent their "Global Hegemony" victory. I'm examining potential alliances and the impact of liberating city-states, considering if our current Diplomacy flavor is sufficient for this tactical shift.
            
            **Focusing Delegate Acquisition**
            
            I'm prioritizing raising my Diplomacy flavor to 100 to flood the board with envoys and capture city-state alliances. Preventing the Zulu's imminent diplomatic victory is paramount, requiring a swift shift to acquire delegates and disrupt their hold before the UN vote in nine turns.
            
            **Securing UN Delegate Votes**
            
            I'm strategizing to sway city-state delegates away from the Zulus. Given Rome's alliance and their independent voting in the World Congress, their delegate count isn't automatically ours. I need to focus on acquiring more city-state alliances to directly counter the Zulu's overwhelming delegate advantage before the upcoming vote.
            
            **Disrupting Zulu Victory**
            
            I'm now focused on surgically reducing the Zulus' delegate count to avert their "Global Hegemony" win. Specifically, I am targeting their city-state alliances, as removing even one would prevent Shaka from securing the necessary votes.
            
            **Liberating City-States**
            
            I'm now exploring the tactical advantage of capturing Ormus. If Rome or I secure it, their alliance breaks, costing the Zulus delegates. This city-state's proximity makes it a prime target to disrupt Shaka's UN victory.
            
            **Conquering Key City-States**
            
            I'm assessing the immediate impact of conquering Ormus; if Rome or I take it, the Zulus lose delegates. I'm also investigating if vassals' delegates count towards their master's UN vote total, which could significantly alter the current diplomatic standings.
            
            **Confirming Diplomatic Victory Status**
            
            My current thoughts are focused on the Zulus' impending diplomatic victory. I've confirmed their delegate count of 43, alongside the votes from Brazil and the Huns, and realized they already possess enough delegates to win.
            
            **Disrupting Delegate Count**
            
            I'm re-evaluating strategies to decrease the Zulus' delegate advantage. Given their overwhelming votes and imminent victory, I'm prioritizing increasing our Diplomacy flavor to secure city-state alliances and considering offensive actions against Ormus, as this could cripple Shaka's UN aspirations.
            
            **Detecting Immediate Atomic Threat**
            
            The Zulus possess three atomic bombs within striking distance of Nippur and Dur-Kurigalzu. My immediate concern is their air force composition and the range of these weapons, necessitating a defensive posture and a re-evaluation of our strategic response to this unprecedented danger.
            
            **Initiating Nuclear Standoff**
            
            I've confirmed an active nuclear standoff. The Zulus have atomic bombs within range of Nippur and Dur-Kurigalzu, but I've rebased two of our own atomic bombs to Nippur. Our "Strategic Defense System" and anti-air units are also in place.
            
            **Targeting Air Force**
            
            My current focus is on eliminating the Zulus' air force. I've identified that nuking Sippar will destroy their atomic bombs, stealth bombers, and jet fighters, especially those rebased on a carrier near the city.
            
            **Targeting Atomic Strike Force**
            
            I'm confirming the Zulus have rebased their atomic bombs and jet fighters to a carrier near Sippar. Nuke the carrier or Sippar itself to destroy their strike capability and atomic bombs, within range of Nippur. My flavors are maxed for nuclear action.
            
            **Targeting Atomic Strike Force**
            
            I'm confirming the Zulus have rebased their atomic bombs and jet fighters to a carrier near Sippar. Nuke the carrier or Sippar itself to destroy their strike capability and atomic bombs, within range of Nippur. My flavors are maxed for nuclear action.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            My thoughts are focused on increasing my `Diplomacy` flavor to 100 to secure city-state alliances and counter the Zulus' UN advantage. I'm also adjusting `Offense` to 40 and `Defense` to 100 to facilitate capturing Ormus while maintaining robust defenses against their nuclear threat.
            
            **Initiating Nuclear Retaliation**
            
            I'm ensuring my `Nuke` and `UseNuke` flavors are at 100 to effectively neutralize the Zulu carrier and their nuclear assets near Sippar, thereby eliminating their immediate atomic threat.
            
            **Securing Science Victory Path**
            
            My focus remains on accelerating the spaceship parts production with `Spaceship`, `Science`, `Production`, and `GreatPeople` flavors at 100, aligned with "Mobile Tactics" research and "Spaceflight Pioneers" policy.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm implementing a critical adjustment to our strategic flavors. Diplomacy and UseNuke are now at 100 to counter the Zulu's UN win and eliminate their atomic threat. Offense is set to 40 for tactical gains, while others like Defense, Production, and Science remain at 100 to maintain overall strength and progress.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            I'm implementing a critical adjustment to our strategic flavors. Diplomacy and UseNuke are now at 100 to counter the Zulu's UN win and eliminate their atomic threat. Offense is set to 40 for tactical gains, while others like Defense, Production, and Science remain at 100 to maintain overall strength and progress.
            
            **Adjusting Flavors and Strategy**
            
            I'm setting Diplomacy and UseNuke to 100 to counter the Zulu UN victory and their atomic threat, while also bolstering Offense to 40 for tactical captures. I'm reinforcing Mobile Tactics research and Spaceflight Pioneers policy to maintain our path to victory.
            
            **Shifting Flavors to Neutralize Threats**
            
            I'm now setting Diplomacy and UseNuke flavors to 100 to aggressively counter the Zulu UN victory and neutralize their atomic strike force near Sippar. Simultaneously, I'm raising Offense to 40 for tactical gains while maintaining Production and Science at 100 for continued progress.
            
            **Neutralizing Immediate Threats**
            
            My immediate focus is on neutralizing the Zulu's diplomatic and atomic threats. I'm allocating flavors to heavily boost diplomat acquisition to counter their UN victory and preparing to use nukes to eliminate their carrier and air force, while preserving our science victory path.
